- Reading: a 15 km neighbour adds under 1 point at every lead up to 30 min, and at most +0.047 (Alameda, 120 min, where the self-only predictor is already worse than persistence at -0.081). At 60 and 120 min the neighbour adds +0.01 to +0.05, always positive but small. A second station in the same bay or sound is not the missing skill for tack planning; the 6zb input stays open and points at offshore upwind sensing or radar nowcasts.
- Limits: two pairs, both in a bay or sound with a land-sea circulation that may dominate; January-to-July transfer for the 15 km pair (Alameda self-only skill goes negative at 120 min, a sign of seasonal drift); first-half-to-second-half July for the 70 km pair (diurnal and synoptic regime shared); linear ridge only; no intervals, since the test windows are autocorrelated and a bootstrap by day would be the next step; skill is vector RMSE, not tack-decision value.
- Next: put the best predictor (San Francisco self-only skill, +0.08) through `sim_replan_dead` at tau 240 s to see whether 8% RMSE skill moves the sail score at all; else paper section 3.

## 2026-11-07 · Milestone 6zc: how much wind-forecast skill do a buoy's own observations carry at 10 to 120 min? (closes 6zb's "Next"; prediction written before the run)
- Why: 6za/6zb left "a forecast with real skill at 15 minutes" as the open input. Before building another replanner run, measure whether any observation-only predictor beats persistence at all. `experiments/run_wind_nowcast_skill.py`: on the 10-minute NDBC series in `experiments/data` (buoys with both January and July 2024), a per-buoy ridge autoregression (6 lags of u and v, correction to persistence) is fitted on January and scored on July at leads 10, 20, 30, 60, 120 min, plus a no-fit damped-trend rule. Metric: vector RMSE, skill = 1 - RMSE/RMSE_persistence.
- **Prediction:** the ridge AR skill is under +0.05 pooled at every lead up to 30 min (prior 70%: 10-minute winds are close to a random walk at one point) and below +0.10 at 120 min (prior 60%); the damped trend is at or below zero at 60 min and beyond (prior 65%). Refuted: any pooled AR skill of +0.05 or more at 30 min or below; +0.10 or more at 120 min; trend above zero at 60 min.
- Limits known in advance: one point per buoy, no upwind information; 10-minute samples (gusts and sub-10-minute variability not seen); January-to-July transfer tests seasonal drift as well as skill.

### Outcome of 6zc (`experiments/results_wind_nowcast_skill.txt`; under 10 s, deterministic; 8 buoys with both months, about 35,500 July test rows per lead)
- **All three parts of the prediction held.** Pooled ridge AR skill over persistence is -0.005, +0.003, +0.010 at 10, 20, 30 min (under +0.05), +0.021 at 60 min and +0.038 at 120 min (under +0.10). The damped trend is worse than persistence at every lead (-0.07 at 10 min to -0.72 at 120 min; 0 of 8 buoys above zero at any lead).
- Reading: a buoy's own past wind adds almost nothing to persistence at the 15 to 30 minute scale that 6zb said was the open input (RMSE per component 0.67 m/s at 10 min, 1.01 at 30 min). The AR gain is consistent in sign by 60 min (8 of 8 buoys) but is 2 to 4%. So the missing 15-minute skill cannot come from a single-point time series; it needs spatial information (upwind sensors, radar nowcast) or a model with data assimilation at that scale.
- Limits: 8 of the 10 buoys (two have one month only); one point per buoy, 10-minute samples, January-to-July transfer; linear predictors only (no nonlinear or learned spatial model). This bounds what observation-only prediction offers; it does not test radar or upwind-sensor nowcasts.
- Next: a spatial predictor needs an upwind pair at under 50 km, which the 10 NDBC buoys do not offer; try CO-OPS coastal station pairs (data already in `experiments/data/coops_wind_*`) or HRRR 15-minute output, then feed whichever has skill to `sim_replan_dead` at tau 240 s.

## 2026-11-07 · Milestone 6zb: does a switch margin rescue HRRR at tau 240 s? (closes 6za's "Next"; prediction written before the run)
- Why: 6za found real HRRR replanning 2.4 to 5.5 points below the header at tau 240 s, with more tacks than persistence (raw L1 66/day against 44). `sim_replan_dead` already takes `margin`: a voluntary tack happens only if the forecast's gain beats margin x one step of best model progress. `experiments/run_sail_hrrr_margin.py` sweeps margin in {0, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8} at tau 240 s for raw and bias-corrected HRRR (L = 1 h) and the synthetic T = 120 min forecast, same 5n windows (every 4th; 72 January, 80 July), header margin 0.5. The margin is read off January and judged on July (a fixed rule, not a July pick).
- **Prediction:** the January-best margin lifts July bias-corrected HRRR L1 to within 1 point of the header (gap better than -1.0; prior 40%), but does not beat it by 1 point or more (prior 85%). Margins cut tacks per day by at least a third at margin 2 (prior 80%). Refuted: gap still below -1.0; or a gain of 1 point or more; or tacks cut by less than a third.
- Limits known in advance: 5e to 5g found margins useless on the synthetic forecast at 30 s tacks; 10 buoys, windows within a month not independent; tau is pure dead time.

### Outcome of 6zb (`experiments/results_sail_hrrr_margin.txt`; about 4 minutes on 4 cores)
- **Prediction: two parts held, one refuted.** January's best margin for bias-corrected HRRR L1 is a tie from 0.5 up (+0.0 points; median share highest at 4 to 8), so margin 4 is the January pick. On July it gives -0.2 points against the header (bootstrap 95% -1.9 to +0.0): inside the -1.0 bar, and no gain of 1 point (**held**, both). Tacks cut by a third at margin 2: **refuted**, bc L1 54 to 44 per day in July (-19%) and 57 to 48 in January (-16%); raw L1 66 to 46 (-30%).
- Raw HRRR L1 goes from -5.5 (margin 0) to -0.9 (margin 1) and -0.1 (margin 4) in July; January -3.2 to -0.2 and +0.0. But no margin makes any HRRR variant beat the header: the best July cell is +0.0. As the margin rises HRRR converges on the header's level at about 42 tacks/day, i.e. the margin does not extract skill, it switches the forecast off. The synthetic T = 120 forecast shows the same: +0.8 at margin 0 falls to +0.0 by margin 1.
- So at a fielded 240 s tack price the useful rule from a real 3 km forecast is "ignore it unless very sure"; the 6z synthetic gains (+7.5 to +11 points at perfect skill, +4.5 at 240-minute decay) need forecast skill that archived HRRR at these buoys lacks, and tuning the tack penalty only recovers the loss.
- Limits: one tau (240 s); margin grid not refined between 0.5 and 4; 10 buoys, every 4th window, within-month windows not independent (intervals optimistic); raw HRRR margin gain is partly mechanical (it removes tacks), not evidence of skill.
- Next: paper section 3 (what would make the wing-sail part submittable); a forecast with real skill at 15 minutes (nowcast radar or buoy trend) is the open input.

## 2026-11-07 · Milestone 6za: does an archived HRRR forecast pay at a fielded tack cost? (closes 6z's "Next"; prediction written after the run was launched but before any output was read)
- Why: 6z found the synthetic lead-decay forecast gains +7.5 to +11 points over the header-follower at tau 120 to 360 s. 5n (tau 30 s) found real HRRR at or below the header. `experiments/run_sail_hrrr_hourly_dead.py` feeds the 5n NDBC-buoy windows (every 4th; 72 January, 80 July) and archived hourly HRRR cycles (L = 1 and 3 h, raw and bias-corrected) to `sim_replan_dead` at tau in {120, 240, 360} s, with T0 and T = 120 min synthetic rows, the header (margin 0.5), corridor and clairvoyant DP.
- **Prediction:** at tau 240 s, July, bias-corrected HRRR (L = 1 h) beats the header by at least 1 point (prior 35%; 5n gave -0.6 at 30 s, but 6z showed forecast value rises with tack cost); raw HRRR still loses to the header (prior 70%). Refuted if bc HRRR is under +1.0, or raw HRRR is at or above the header.

### Outcome of 6za (`experiments/results_sail_hrrr_hourly_dead.txt`; about 8 minutes on 4 cores)
- **Both parts of the prediction go the pessimistic way for HRRR: (1) refuted, (2) held.** July, tau 240 s: bc HRRR L1 is -2.4 points against the header (95% -4.8 to -1.0), raw HRRR L1 -5.5 (-6.7 to -3.7), L3 -4.0. Same sign at tau 120 and 360 s and in January (raw -2.4 to -3.4, bc -0.5 to -0.6).
- Raising the tack price does not rescue HRRR: its gap to the header stays at -2 to -5 points while the synthetic T = 120 min forecast gains +0.7 to +1.6. HRRR forecasts also tack more (raw L1 66 per day at 240 s against 44 for persistence), and every extra tack costs 240 s.
- Persistence (T0) ties the header (-0.1 to +0.3). The clairvoyant stays near 0.99, so headroom is 18 to 22 points; the 6z synthetic forecast gains are not reached by a real 3 km forecast on these windows.
- Limits: 10 NDBC buoys, every 4th window, windows within a month not independent (intervals optimistic); buoy anemometer height not corrected; tau is pure dead time; header margin fixed at 0.5; not a test of newer or ensemble forecasts. The prediction was written after launching the job, before reading output.
- Next: a forecast-aware tack penalty (replan only when forecast gain exceeds a multiple of tau), then paper section 3.

## 2026-11-07 · Milestone 6z: does the value of a wind forecast (5d) rise or fall when a tack costs the fielded 120 to 360 s? (closes 6y's "Next"; prediction written before the run)
- Why: 5d showed hourly replanning on a forecast beats the header-follower by 3 to 5 points at a 30 s tack. 6x found the header's own edge over plain tacking vanishes by 360 s and the clairvoyant gap grows (16 to 26 points). 6y left the replanners unable to carry dead time. Now `tack.dp_values_dead` and `tack.sim_replan_dead` (tack = whole steps of dead time) are added, with tests (`tests/test_replan_dead.py`: the value equals `dp_clairvoyant_dead` with a corridor too wide to reach; one perfect-forecast plan lies between the corridor tacker and the DP). `experiments/run_sail_lead_dead.py` reruns 5d (every 4th window, hourly replan, T in {0, 60, 240, inf} min) at tau in {120, 240, 360} s against the header (margin 0.5) and corridor tackers and the clairvoyant DP.
- **Prediction:** at tau 240 s, July, the perfect-smoothed forecast (T = inf) beats the header-follower by at least 6 points (5d: +5.4 at 30 s), because a costly tack makes knowing which ones to skip worth more; and persistence (T = 0) at least matches the header (>= 0.0 points; 5d: -2.6). Prior 60% for the first, 50% for the second. Refuted if T = inf gains under 6 points, or persistence loses to the header.
- Limits known in advance: same 24 stations and windows as 5d, a subset (every 4th); the forecast is the 5d synthetic lead-decay one, not HRRR; tau is pure dead time (6x's caveat); the DP grid dy = 25 m.

### Outcome of 6z (`experiments/results_sail_lead_dead.txt`; 96 windows per month, about 12 minutes on 4 cores)
- **Prediction (1) held:** July, tau 240 s, the perfect-smoothed forecast (T = inf) beats the header-follower by +8.7 points (bootstrap 95% 7.5 to 10.4), against 6 required; 5d had +5.4 at 30 s. Gain by tau 120 / 240 / 360 s: +7.5 / +8.7 / +11.0 (January +5.8 / +6.7 / +8.4). Forecast value rises with tack cost, because the header rule loses its edge (6x) while the planner skips tacks (tacks/day 50 / 39 / 34 against the header's much larger count).
- **Prediction (2) refuted:** persistence (T = 0) at tau 240 s, July, is -0.6 points against the header (95% -2.0 to -0.1); it ties at 360 s (+0.2, interval -1.4 to +1.6) and loses at 120 s (-1.1). January shows +0.3 to +1.3. So no-skill persistence only matches the header; skill is what pays.
- Skill ladder at tau 240 s, July: persistence -0.6, 60-min decay +2.4, 240-min decay +4.5, perfect +8.7 points; the clairvoyant stays 13 to 14 points above the perfect-smoothed forecast (0.987 vs 0.856), the part due to 1-minute detail the 15-min smoothing removes.
- Limits: synthetic lead-decay forecast, not HRRR (the HRRR runs 5h to 5n are still at 30 s tacks); every 4th window; tau is pure dead time (6x); January chose nothing here (header margin fixed at 6x's 0.5); windows within a month are not independent, so intervals are optimistic.
- Next: the HRRR forecast at tau 240 s (needs dead-time forecasts through `sim_replan_dead`; the data are in `experiments/data/`); then paper section 3.

## 2026-11-07 · Milestone 6y: close the silent tack-cost clipping in the remaining controllers, and fold 6w and 6x into the paper (a code-safety and writing step, no experiment)
- 6x found that every controller clipped a tack longer than one step to one step. `sim_corridor`, `sim_header` and the clairvoyant DP were fixed there. `dp_values`, `sim_policy`, `sim_replan` and `sim_mpc` (the forecast-lead controllers of 5b to 5g and 5h to 5n) model a tack as a share of one step or block and cannot carry dead time, so they now raise `ValueError` when tau exceeds that span (`_require_short_tack`) instead of returning a wrong number. For tau at or below 60 s (all of 5a to 6) behaviour is unchanged: the 87 tests in `tests/` pass, including a new one that the four refuse tau = 120 s (and `sim_mpc` 400 s).
- Paper `DRAFT.md` section 2.5 gains the 6x row (6w was already there): at a fielded tack price the header-follower's edge over plain corridor tacking goes from +3.2 points (30 s) to +0.0 (360 s).
- Not done, and still the real open item: carry dead time through `dp_values`/`sim_replan` (a state for tack time remaining) and rerun 5d and the HRRR tests at tau 120 to 360 s, to see whether forecast value rises with tau. No result is claimed for it.
- Next: that dead-time state; then paper section 3 (what would make it submittable).

## 2026-11-06 · Milestone 6x: do the tack controllers' ranking and the header gain survive a measured tack cost of 120 to 360 s? (closes 6w's "Next"; prediction written before the run)
- Why: 5a to 5g priced a tack at tau in {0, 30, 60} s; 6w measured an effective 218 s (127 to 301) from fielded Saildrone blocks, 373 s matched on angle. `experiments/run_sail_tack_long.py` reruns 5a's corridor (A), header-follower (B) and clairvoyant DP on the same 24 stations x 2 months of CO-OPS wind, same windows, at tau in {120, 240, 360} s. The header margin is chosen on January at tau 240 s from {0.3, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0} (5a's winning 0.3 was at the grid's edge-ish low tau; costlier tacks may want a larger margin) and reported on July.
- **Prediction:** (1) the July header-minus-corridor gain at tau 240 s is at least 5 points (5a: 3.2 at 30 s, 2.6 at 60 s: it was flat to falling, so I expect the corridor's ~85 tacks/day to cost it more than the header's fewer ones); (2) the header beats the corridor in at least 90% of July windows at 240 s; (3) the clairvoyant minus header gap stays above 10 points. Prior 50% for (1), 75% for (2), 85% for (3). (1) is refuted if the gain is below 5 points; the ranking is reversed if the header loses at any tau.
- Limits known in advance: the controllers see the same 1-minute wind as in 5a; tau is a time of zero progress per tack, whereas 6w's figure is an effective loss over a 20-minute window including selection.

### Outcome of 6x (`experiments/results_sail_tack_long.txt`; 24 stations, July holdout 360 windows, margin 0.5 chosen on January at 240 s)
- **Library bug found first.** The first run gave identical shares at tau 120, 240 and 360, equal to 5a's tau = 60 row. Cause: every controller in `src/marine_hull/tack.py` set the tack penalty to `min(1, tau/dt)` with dt = 60 s, so any tau above 60 s was silently 60 s. Fixed for `sim_corridor`, `sim_header` (penalty carried across steps) and a new `dp_clairvoyant_dead` (integer dead steps); for tau <= 60 s the code is unchanged, so 5a to 5g are unaffected (they never went above 60 s). `tests/test_tack_long.py` checks the penalty is no longer clipped and that the new DP equals the old one at one step. The clipped output was not kept. `sim_mpc`, `sim_policy`, `sim_replan` and `dp_values` still clip at one step (or one block); they must be fixed before use at tau above their step.
- **Prediction (1) refuted:** July header-minus-corridor gain is +2.3, +0.8 and +0.0 points at tau 120, 240 and 360 s (bootstrap 95% at 240 s: 0.36 to 1.40), not 5 or more. The gain falls with tau; I expected it to rise.
- **Prediction (2) refuted:** the header beats the corridor in 274, 218 and 177 of 360 July windows (76%, 61%, 49%), against 5a's 327 of 360 at 30 s. Stations with a positive median gain: 12, 10, 7 of 12.
- **Prediction (3) held, and understated:** clairvoyant minus header is 16.2, 21.0, 26.2 points (5a: 12.1 at 30 s). The headroom for a better controller grows with tack cost.
- Levels: July corridor / header / clairvoyant shares are 0.799 / 0.827 / 0.992 at 120 s, 0.768 / 0.773 / 0.988 at 240 s, 0.735 / 0.716 / 0.985 at 360 s. At 6w's measured 218 to 373 s the header-follower no longer reliably beats plain edge tacking, so 5a's "header-following is worth about 3 points" does not transfer to a fielded-cost tack; what pays is knowing the wind ahead (the clairvoyant gap), which is the forecast-lead question of 5b to 5g, now at a tack price near 240 s.
- Limits: tau here is pure dead time per tack, while 6w's 218 to 373 s is an effective loss over a 20-minute window including selection, so these are bracketing values, not the fielded price; margin grid 0.3 to 2.0 chosen on January at 240 s only (0.5 won; 1.0 and 2.0 are identical because they never trigger a header tack); header controller sees the same 1-minute wind as 5a; January/July windows are not independent, so the bootstrap interval is optimistic.
- Next: rerun the forecast-lead controllers (5b to 5g) after fixing the clipping in `sim_mpc`/`sim_policy`/`sim_replan` to see whether forecast value rises with tau; paper section 2.5 row.

## 2026-11-06 · Milestone 6w: what does a tack cost a fielded Saildrone? (closes 6v's "Next"; prediction written before the run)
- Why: milestones 5a to 5g and 6 priced tacks at tau in {0, 30, 60} s of no progress, all swept, none measured. The fielded 10-minute Saildrone blocks (`saildrone_10min.csv.gz`, 8 drones, summers 2018 and 2019) hold heading, course, speed and the drone's own true wind, so tack events can be found and their windward progress compared with steady upwind blocks.
- Plan (`experiments/run_saildrone_tack_cost.py`): relative angle rel = HDG - wind-from (wind-from = atan2(-u,-v)). Upwind-sailing block: true wind >= 4 m/s and 30 <= |rel| <= 80 deg. A tack pair is two consecutive 10-minute blocks of one drone, both upwind-sailing, with opposite sign of rel; a steady pair has the same sign. Windward VMG = SOG cos(COG - wind-from). Within wind-speed bins (4 to 6, 6 to 8, 8 to 10, 10 to 14 m/s), the loss of a tack = 1200 s x (1 - mean VMG of tack pairs / mean VMG of steady pairs), in seconds of full-speed progress. 95% interval from a bootstrap over drone-days.
- **Prediction:** the pooled median tack loss lies between 0 and 60 s (the model's swept range), prior 55%. Refuted if the pooled estimate is above 60 s or its interval excludes 0 on the negative side (tack pairs faster than steady pairs).
- Limits known in advance: 10-minute blocks put the manoeuvre inside an averaged block, so a short tack is a small dip in a block mean, and a pair of blocks is a 20-minute window; tacks are chosen by the drone's controller, not at random (selection); heading mean over a block that contains a tack is a circular mean; wind from the drone's own anemometer.

### Outcome of 6w (`experiments/results_saildrone_tack_cost.txt`; 24,555 consecutive upwind block pairs, 1,147 tack pairs, 670 drone-days, under 10 s)
- **Prediction refuted.** Pooled effective loss per tack is 218 s (bootstrap 95% interval 127 to 301 s), against a swept 0 to 60 s. Matching on sailing angle (|rel| 30-45, 45-60, 60-80 deg inside each wind bin) raises it to 373 s (300 to 439 s), so the angle difference between tack and steady pairs (50.1 against 53.4 deg) does not explain it. It falls with wind: 326, 258, 135, 116 s in the 4-6, 6-8, 8-10, 10-14 m/s bins.
- Reading: in the fielded data a tack costs the windward progress of several minutes, not 30 to 60 s. This is an effective loss over a 20-minute window, not the manoeuvre alone: it includes the drone's recovery to speed, and the controller tacks when wind shifts or lulls, which lowers VMG regardless (selection). So it is an upper bound on the manoeuvre and a fair price of tacking as the fielded controller does it. The 5a to 5g and 6 tack results (header-following, forecast lead, corridor width) priced tacks at 0 to 60 s; at 200 to 370 s tacking less often is worth more than those runs found, so their "what control is worth" figures are optimistic about frequent tacking.
- Limits: 10-minute blocks, selection as above, a loss defined against steady pairs of the same wind and angle bin, summers 2018 and 2019 on the US West Coast, drone's own anemometer.
- Next: rerun the corridor tack controllers (`run_sail_tack.py`) at tau = 120, 240, 360 s to see whether the controller ranking and the wind-over-sun ratios move; paper section 2.5 row.

## 2026-11-06 · Milestone 6v: is 6s's scalar drift c = 0.48 m/s a coherent current? (closes 6t's "Next"; exploratory, no prediction logged first)
- `experiments/run_saildrone_current_vector.py` -> `results_saildrone_current_vector.txt` (under 10 s): in near-calm blocks (true wind < 1 m/s: 3,224 in 2018, 1,735 in 2019) the sail gives no thrust, so SOG east/north is current plus wave drift. A coherent current would show as a large mean vector; random drift as a mean near zero beside the spread.
- Result: the year-level mean vector is 0.011 m/s (2018) and 0.042 m/s (2019) against a p90 SOG of 0.48 and 0.50: the coherent share of squared SOG is 0.1% and 1.2%. By drone-day (>= 6 calm blocks; 170 and 89 days) the median daily mean has magnitude 0.18 to 0.20 m/s and a median coherent share of 0.35 to 0.37, so a part is a slowly varying current. Removing each drone-day's own mean vector only lowers the p90 from 0.47 to 0.40 (2018) and 0.50 to 0.44 (2019). Along-heading mean +0.09 and +0.15 m/s (cross-heading -0.03): a small forward creep.
- Reading: 6s's c is mostly zero-mean scatter (direction changes block to block), not a steady current. A heading-aware current from OSCAR or CO-OPS would explain at most the daily-coherent third, and even a perfect daily current only trims the p90 by about 15%. So 6t's July band (0.71 to 0.93 times solar, 0.79 central) stands, and the planned OSCAR vector step is of small value for this question; it is closed rather than done.
- Limits: calm blocks are a selected state (the drone may be station-keeping or drifting under towing/rudder motion); 10-minute SOG from GPS carries noise of its own that I did not separate from drift; two summers, US West Coast; wind below 1 m/s taken from the drone's own anemometer.
- Next: paper section 2.5 note; the remaining open item for H-wind is a measured tack cost, or a different corridor than the US West Coast.

## 2026-11-06 · Milestone 6u: paper section 2.5 gains 6s and 6t (a writing step, no experiment)
- Added the 6s drift calibration (c = 0.48 m/s; light-air coverage 0.545 -> 0.962) and 6t drift-aware estimate (July 0.79, January 1.43 times solar) to `paper/DRAFT.md` rows and the quotable band (July 0.71 to 0.93, January 1.40 to 1.47). Numbers copied from the 6s/6t entries; no new run.
- Next: heading-aware current vector from OSCAR/CO-OPS data in `experiments/data/`.

## 2026-11-06 · Milestone 6t: a drift-aware central estimate between 6r's floor and ceiling (closes 6s's "Next"; prediction written before the run)
- Why: 6r credited light-air (< 4 m/s) blocks either fully (6n drone, a ceiling) or not at all (floor). 6s showed the drift c = 0.48 m/s explains most light-air SOG, and that a drone refitted with c in the model (sail_area 0.68 m2, hull_scale 4.05) predicts sail-driven speed there with the drift kept out of the credited distance. `experiments/run_wind_vs_sun_drift.py` uses 6n's drone at >= 4 m/s and the 6s-fitted sail-only drone below 4 m/s, same buoys, corridors and solar ceiling as 6r.
- **Prediction:** July median ratio to the 86 km/day solar ceiling lies between 6r's floor (0.71) and ceiling (0.93), and below 0.82 (closer to the floor, because hull_scale 4 makes light-air thrust small); January median stays at or above 1.35. Prior 65%. Refuted if July is >= 0.82 or January < 1.35.

### Outcome of 6t (`experiments/results_wind_vs_sun_drift.txt`; 19 buoy-months, under 1 minute)
- **Prediction held.** July median 68 km/day = **0.79 x solar** (ceiling 0.93, floor 0.71); drift-aware reaches the solar ceiling at 1 of 10 buoys (same as the ceiling). January median 123 km/day = 1.43 x solar (ceiling 1.47, floor 1.40), at or above solar at 9 of 9.
- Reading: the winter edge is robust to any light-air treatment (1.40 to 1.47 x). July is a band, 0.71 to 0.93 x, with 0.79 x as the central value under a drone whose light-air drag is fitted to fielded speeds with drift kept out. The paper can say "wind is below sun in July (0.7 to 0.9 x), ahead of it in January (1.4 to 1.5 x)".
- Limits: the light-air drone (hull_scale 4.05) is a curve fit to p90 SOG with a scalar drift, not a physical hull; same two months and 19 buoy-months; zero tack cost; no sea state; the 86 km/day solar ceiling is itself a bound.
- Next: a heading-aware current vector from the OSCAR/CO-OPS data in `experiments/data/`; or fold 6r to 6t into paper section 2.5's table.

## 2026-11-06 · Milestone 6s: does a current term fix the light-air coverage 6q could not? (closes 6r's "Next"; exploratory, no prediction logged first)
- `experiments/run_saildrone_current.py` -> `results_saildrone_current.txt` (under 1 minute): model p90 SOG = drone speed + c, with c the 90th-percentile SOG of near-calm 2018 blocks (true wind < 1 m/s; 3,224 blocks), read from the data and not fitted. Drone parameters refitted on 2018 light-air cells (96 cells, 33,680 blocks) with c included; coverage judged on 2019 light-air blocks (18,921).
- Result: c = 0.478 m/s (2019 calm blocks: 0.499, so c transfers across years). 6n's drone with c added covers **0.962** of 2019 light-air blocks (0.545 without): 0.935 at 0-2 m/s (0.245 before), 0.979 at 2-3, 0.975 at 3-4. A bound that is right covers about 0.90, so it is slightly conservative, not short. Refitting sail_area and hull_scale with c gives 0.68 m2 and 4.05 (MSE 0.0047 against 0.053 in 6q) but covers only 0.829: it chases the cell means and loses the bound; hull_scale 4.05 is at the top of the search grid and not physical.
- Reading: 6q's diagnosis holds. In light air a near-constant 0.5 m/s of drift and current, not sail thrust, explains most of the SOG that beat the model. For the paper: the July wind figure should be stated as a ceiling, or computed on blocks at or above 4 m/s plus a drift term that is not credited as sail distance (6r's floor already does that).
- Limits: c is a scalar added to speed, whereas current is a vector along an unknown heading (the 90th percentile of |SOG| in calm air is an upper-end magnitude); near-calm Saildrone motion may include wave drift and station-keeping; US West Coast, two summers; SOG, not speed through water.
- Next: use the 0.48 m/s drift to give 6r's floor a drift-aware companion; or add the OSCAR/CO-OPS currents already in `experiments/data/` as a heading-aware vector.

## 2026-11-06 · Milestone 6r: how much of 6o's wind-over-sun ranking rests on light-air hours? (closes 6q's "Next"; exploratory, no prediction logged first)
- `experiments/run_wind_vs_sun_lightair_share.py` -> `results_wind_vs_sun_lightair_share.txt` (under 2 minutes): 6o's capability drone and buoy winds again (reproduces 6o's 126 and 80 km/day medians), reporting per buoy-month the share of blocks below 4 m/s, the share of modelled distance they supply, and the corridor mean with those blocks credited zero distance (a floor for the case that light-air speed over ground is current, not sail thrust, as 6q found).
- Result: January: median 17% of blocks below 4 m/s supply 6% of distance; zeroing them leaves 120 km/day (1.40 times the 86 km/day solar ceiling), above solar in 9 of 9 buoys. July: median 44% of blocks below 4 m/s supply 24% of distance; modelled 80 km/day (0.93 times) falls to 61 (0.71 times) when zeroed, above solar in 1 of 10 buoys.
- Reading: the winter edge does not depend on light air. The July "level with solar" figure does: it is a ceiling for the sail side, and with light-air thrust removed wind is below sun at 9 of 10 buoys. So the paper's July claim is "wind is at most level with sun, and below it if light-air speed is current-driven". The direction of the ranking is unchanged; the July number moves down, not up.
- Limits: zeroing is a deliberately pessimistic floor (some light-air sail thrust is real); same two months, 19 buoy-months, zero tack cost, SOG-fitted drone, no sea state.
- Next: a current term in the drone model, refit on 2018 and tested on 2019 light-air blocks.

## 2026-11-06 · Milestone 6q: does a light-air-only capability fit cover 2019 blocks below 4 m/s? (closes 6o's "Next"; exploratory, predictions not logged before the run)
- `experiments/run_saildrone_lightair.py` -> `results_saildrone_lightair.txt` (under 1 s): fit sail_area and hull_scale to the 2018 90th-percentile SOG in 0.5 m/s x 15 deg cells (>= 20 blocks, 96 cells, 33,680 blocks, true wind < 4 m/s only); test coverage on 18,921 light-air 2019 blocks.
- Result: the fit goes to sail_area 0.30 m2, hull_scale 0.30 (MSE 0.053 against 0.059 for 6n's drone): hull drag at 0.3 of the still-water model is not physical, and the gain is small. Coverage (a bound that is right covers ~0.90): 0.645 overall against 0.545 for 6n's drone; by wind 0-2 m/s 0.34 (0.25), 2-3 m/s 0.77 (0.67), 3-4 m/s 0.84 (0.75).
- Reading: refitting does not rescue the model in light air. Below 2 m/s two thirds of 2019 blocks go faster than even the light-air fit, so SOG there is mostly current and drift (the model has no current), not sail thrust. Do not read the 6o July result (0.93 times solar) as a drone bound for light-air buoys; it is an upper-end sail estimate on a regime where speed over ground is not sail-driven. This weakens, not strengthens, any claim about July.
- Limits: SOG not speed through water; the 90th percentile is a choice; two years of US West Coast data; the fit hit the edge of a hand-chosen grid's physical range, so the parameters are a curve fit, not a drone.
- Next: add a current term (the cells' drift speed from near-zero-wind blocks) to the model and refit; or restrict the paper's July claim to winds >= 4 m/s.

## 2026-11-06 · Milestone 6p: 6h to 6o folded into one paper section (a writing step, no experiment)
- Replaced the single 6h bullet in `paper/DRAFT.md` section 2.5 with a table of the seven steps (default drone, corridor, tack loss, one fielded mean, 864 fielded drone-days, fitted mission drone, capability drone) and the sentence the paper can support: with a drone model that reproduces fielded speeds, wind beats sun by 1.2 to 1.5 times in January and is level or below in July (0.72 to 0.93 times). Every figure is copied from 6h to 6o above and the result files named; nothing was re-run.
- Limits carried over: the capability fit covers 68% of 2019 blocks, not 90%; area and hull drag unidentified separately; solar ceiling is itself a bound; no sea state; two months; Explorer 7 m data against a 5 m model.
- Next: light-air-specific fit (wind below 4 m/s, finer bins), where 6n's coverage fails and the July buoys sit.

## 2026-11-06 · Milestone 6o: does the wind-over-sun ranking survive the capability drone? (replaces 6n's arithmetic with a run; exploratory, no prediction logged first)
- Why: 6n ended with "a 1.14x model on 6m's medians would be ~140 and ~90 km/day; not re-run". `experiments/run_wind_vs_sun_capability.py` -> `results_wind_vs_sun_capability.txt` (36 s) re-runs 6h/6i (same NDBC buoy winds, four lines both ways, zero tack cost) with the 6n capability drone (sail_area 0.90 m2, hull_scale 1.20).
- Result: corridor median 126 km/day in January (min 104; 1.47 times the 86 km/day solar ceiling) and 80 in July (min 45; 0.93 times). Free beam reach median 158 (min 130) and 102 (min 58). Corridor/free 0.80 and 0.79. Share of days at or above 86 km/day: median 0.81 (min 0.63) January, 0.40 (min 0.07) July. No buoy-month reaches 2x solar (0 of 19; 6i's unfitted drone reached it in 15).
- Reading: against 6m's mission drone (100 and 62) the capability drone is 1.26 and 1.29 times faster, more than 6n's rough 1.14 (the buoy winds are lighter than the Saildrone cells where 1.14 was measured). Even a drone allowed its top-decile speed beats the solar ceiling clearly only in winter; in July it is level with it (0.93 times) and below it on 60% of days. Direction of the 6m ranking holds; the size of the winter edge is 1.2 to 1.5 times, bracketed by the two drones.
- Limits: the capability drone covers only 68% of 2019 blocks (6n) so is not a true bound in light air, which is where July buoys sit; zero tack cost; SOG includes current; buoy anemometer heights uncorrected; the solar ceiling is itself an upper bound; two months.
- Next: fold 6l to 6o into one paper section; light-air-specific fit (wind below 4 m/s, finer bins).

## 2026-11-06 · Milestone 6n-b: independent replication of 6n's capability fit, with an in-sample coverage check (exploratory)
- A parallel session landed 6n first. `experiments/run_saildrone_cap.py` -> `results_saildrone_cap.txt` is a second implementation of the same fit (p90 SOG per cell, 2018; two parameters). It lands in the same valley (area and hull drag trade off; best grid point sail_area 0.45 m2, hull_scale 0.60, MSE 0.055 against 1.62 for the default). Both agree that the fit cannot be a capability bound: 31% of 2019 blocks (46% below 4 m/s, 23% at 4 m/s and above) exceed the curve where 10% should, and the in-sample 2018 share is 25%, so the shortfall is the model's shape, not a year effect. Capability is about 1.24 to 1.31 times the 6m mean-fit speed in every wind band.
- Limits as in 6n; SOG includes current, which dominates below 4 m/s.

## 2026-11-06 · Milestone 6n: what capability (not mission) speed does the fitted drone have? Fit to the 90th-percentile SOG per cell, 2018 -> 2019 (closes 6m's "Next"; pre-registration)
- Why: 6m fitted cell MEANS, which include survey legs, station keeping and noise limits, so its 0.45 m2 is a mission drone. `experiments/run_saildrone_capability.py` -> `results_saildrone_capability.txt` fits the same two parameters (sail_area, hull_scale; same grid and refinement) to the n-weighted 90th-percentile block SOG of each 2018 cell (>= 30 blocks), then on 2019 blocks reports (a) the share of blocks with SOG at or below the model speed (a bound that is right covers about 0.90, by wind), (b) model/observed daily distance on 2019 drone-days, and (c) the fastest-quartile days' observed distance against the model.
- Predictions (written before the run; prior in brackets): P1 the capability fit picks a larger sail_area than 6m's 0.45 m2 [75%]. P2 2019 block coverage is within 0.85 to 0.95 overall [60%]. P3 coverage in winds below 4 m/s is below 0.85, i.e. the bound is not conservative in light air [40%]. P4 the capability drone's 2019 daily distance median is above observed (model/observed 1.2 to 2.0) [60%]. Refuted otherwise.
- Limits (known before running): block SOG includes current (SOG, not speed through water), which the model ignores; the 90th percentile is a choice (the script also prints 75th and 95th coverage for the fitted point); same two-year US West Coast data; sail.py's other parameters unsourced.

### Outcome of 6n (`experiments/results_saildrone_capability.txt`, deterministic, about 8 minutes; the grid header still says "cell means", it is the 90th-percentile target)
- **P1 held:** the capability fit picks sail_area 0.90 m2, hull_scale 1.20 (MSE 0.056 against 1.62 for the default), twice 6m's 0.45 m2. The grid again has a valley (1, 1.5), (1.5, 2), (3, 4) within 0.01 to 0.02.
- **P2 refuted:** 2019 block coverage is 0.68 overall, under the 0.85 to 0.95 band. **P3 held** and is the cause: 0.54 below 4 m/s against 0.75 to 0.76 above. The 2018 fit does not carry a 90th-percentile bound to 2019 in light air (2019 drones sailed faster there than 2018's top decile, or the wind-from-COG cells are sparse); the model is a bound for 68% of blocks, not 90%.
- **P4 refuted:** 2019 daily distance model/observed median 1.14 (10th 0.90, 90th 1.51), under the predicted 1.2 to 2.0, flat across winds (1.06 to 1.21). Fastest-quartile days (82, observed median 151 km/day): 1.11. Median absolute daily error 0.17, a little above 6m's mean-fit 0.15 (expected: it targets a bound, not the mean) and below the empirical lookup's 0.28 (it overshoots 1.28 because top-decile cells are bound-like).
- Reading: a capability drone is about 2 times 6m's sail area, and over a day it exceeds the mission drone by only ~25% (1.14 against 0.89). So the mission/capability gap is small in daily distance; the 6m ranking (wind about equal to the 86 km/day solar ceiling, better in January, worse in July) is not moved by more than that margin even with a capability drone: a 1.14x model on 6m's 125 and 79 km/day free-course medians would be at most ~140 and ~90, i.e. 1.6 and 1.0 times solar. That scaling is rough and was not re-run through 6h to 6j.
- Limits: SOG includes current; 90th percentile is a choice; US West Coast, two summers; light-air coverage is poor; area and hull drag not separately identified; the final scaling is arithmetic, not a re-run.
- Next: re-run 6h/6i with the 0.90 m2 drone to replace the arithmetic; fold 6l to 6n into the paper draft as one section; try a light-air specific cell (wind below 4 m/s) with finer wind bins.

## 2026-11-06 · Milestone 6m: does a fitted drone model (fit on 2018, tested on 2019) restore a wind-over-sun ranking? (closes 6l's "Next"; exploratory, no prediction logged first)
- Why: 6l left a scalar (0.33) that was blunt: observed/model rose from 0.28 in light wind to 0.60 above 10 m/s. `experiments/run_saildrone_fit.py` -> `results_saildrone_fit.txt` (about 8 minutes) fits two `sail.Drone` parameters, sail area and a hull-resistance multiplier, to the n-weighted cell means of SOG (cells: 1 m/s of wind x 15 deg of true wind angle from COG; 158 cells with 30+ blocks) on 2018 only, then scores the 2019 drone-days (329, at least 130 of 144 blocks). References: the default drone, the 0.33 scalar, and the empirical 2018 cell-mean lookup.
- Result: the fit chooses sail_area 0.45 m2 (default 5) and hull_scale 1.0; mean-square error on 2018 cells 0.026 (m/s)^2 against 2.40 for the default. The grid has a valley: (1, 2), (1.5, 3), (2, 4) and (3, 6) are within 0.04 of the best, so area and hull drag are not separately identified; only their combination is. On 2019, model/observed daily distance: default median 2.39 (10th 1.69, 90th 3.46); fitted 0.89 (0.68 to 1.18), flat across winds (0.81 below 4 m/s, 0.90 to 0.95 above); scalar 0.79; empirical lookup 0.96 (0.81 to 1.23). Median absolute daily error: fitted 0.15, scalar 0.24, empirical 0.10. The fit under-predicts light-wind days (0.81) a little, and generalises to the unseen year.
- Re-run of 6h and 6i with the fitted drone (`run_wind_vs_sun_fitted.py`, `run_wind_vs_sun_corridor_fitted.py`, same buoy winds, no further scalar): free beam-reach median 125 km/day in January (min 103) and 79 in July (min 44); corridor (four lines both ways) median 100 km/day January (min 82), 62 July (min 34), corridor/free 0.80 and 0.79. Against the 86 km/day solar ceiling: January 1.16 times, July 0.72 times. Share of days at or above 86 km/day: median 0.61 (January) and 0.17 (July). No buoy-month reaches 2x solar (6i: 15 of 19). Tack-time penalty (6j) not re-run: it only lowers these.
- Reading: with a drone model that reproduces fielded survey speeds, wind beats sun by a thin margin in January and loses in July at open-water buoys. 6h's "2 to 3 times" is gone and the sign now depends on the season; it is also the first version tested on data it was not fitted to. The ranking is conditional on the solar ceiling (86 km/day, itself an upper bound), so it does not settle which energy source is better; it says the wind drone has no large edge at these sites.
- Limits: the fitted 0.45 m2 is an effective area, not the Explorer's wing; it absorbs mission behaviour (survey legs, station keeping, noise-limited speed) and so is a survey-mission drone, probably low for a drone sailing flat out; area and hull drag are not separately identified; Explorers (7 m) on the US West Coast in two summers, applied to Atlantic, Gulf and Pacific buoys in January and July; buoy anemometer heights (4 to 5 m) differ from the drone's measurement height and were not corrected; the 15-degree and 1 m/s cells are coarse; the fit cell means under 30 blocks were dropped; sail.py's other parameters remain unsourced.
- Next: fit separately by season of use or by mission type (transit versus survey blocks, e.g. the top decile of SOG per cell as a capability bound) to give the wind drone a capability, not a mission, speed; fold into the paper draft.

## 2026-11-05 · Milestone 6l: what do 864 fielded Saildrone-days say about the drone model's daily distance? (closes 6k's "Next"; predictions written before the run, logged after)
- Why: 6k had one press-release mean. `experiments/fetch_saildrone.py` pulls the NOAA PMEL ERDDAP west-coast survey tracks (CC0; 2018: five drones, 2019: eight; 60 s position, SOG, COG, and each drone's own true-wind components), thinned to 10-minute blocks in `data/saildrone_10min.csv.gz` (131,535 blocks). `experiments/run_saildrone_daily.py` -> `results_saildrone_daily.txt` (about 1 minute): per drone-day with at least 130 of 144 blocks, observed path length (sum SOG x 600 s) against the `sail.py` default drone (a) at the measured wind and the course actually sailed (true wind angle from COG), and (b) on a free beam reach at the measured wind, which is what 6h used for the buoys.
- Predictions (before the run; prior in brackets): P1 median observed/(a) is 0.8 or below [70%]. P2 median observed/(b) lies in 0.4 to 0.8 [65%]. P3 scaling the 6i/6j buoy corridor medians by that median leaves the July median above the 86 km/day solar ceiling [55%]. Refuted otherwise.
- Result: 864 drone-days, 13 drones. Observed median 75 km/day (10th 35, 90th 156) at a median daily mean wind of 4.7 m/s. P1 held: observed/(a) median 0.40 (10th 0.27, 90th 0.56). P2 refuted: observed/(b) median 0.33 (10th 0.23, 90th 0.53), under the predicted band; it rises with wind, 0.28 below 4 m/s to 0.60 above 10 m/s. P3 refuted: scaled by 0.33, the corridor medians are 79 km/day in January and 60 in July, 0.92 and 0.69 of the solar ceiling; even the free-course January median scales to 91, only 1.06 times it.
- Capability check (same script): per 10-minute block, SOG over the model speed at the measured wind and COG. At 4 to 12 m/s the 99th percentile block is 0.68 to 0.82 of the model, so the real drones never sail at the model speed in moderate wind, which makes the gap a model fault and not only mission behaviour (survey legs, station keeping). Only above 14 m/s do the best blocks reach 1.0.
- Reading: the 5 m default drone is optimistic by about 2.5 to 3 times at survey winds, and 6h's headline ("wind drone 2 to 3 times the solar boat's ceiling") reverses: with this fielded factor the wind drone's buoy-wind distance is at or below the 86 km/day solar ceiling in July and about equal in January. 6k's "fielded mean still 1.3 times the solar ceiling" holds only for the Southern Ocean circumnavigation; on the survey fleet the median day, 75 km/day, is below it. The solar ceiling is itself an upper bound, so the ranking is now open, not reversed.
- Limits: survey missions, so distance includes line turns, station keeping, echo-sounder noise limits and operator routing, none in the model; drones are Saildrone Explorers (7 m, not 5 m, wing area not matched to `sail.py`); the model's sail and hull parameters are still unsourced; the wind is the drone's own 10-minute mean true wind, assumed ground-referenced as documented and not corrected for height; two summers on the US West Coast, not the 19 buoy-months; the 0.33 factor is a median over mixed conditions and varies from 0.28 to 0.60 with wind, so a single scalar is a blunt correction.
- Next: replace the scalar with a fitted model: calibrate sail lift and hull drag so `sail.py` reproduces the block-level SOG-vs-wind curve (fit on 2018, test on 2019), then re-run 6h to 6j with the calibrated drone and compare against the solar ceiling.

## 2026-11-05 · Milestone 6k: does the sail-drone model agree with a fielded drone's published distance? (closes 6i/6j's "Next"; exploratory, prediction formed before the run but not logged first)
- Why: 6h to 6j rank wind over sun using an unsourced 5 m drone model; the open check was a published daily distance. Source: Saildrone's release for SD 1020, a 7 m Explorer, which circled Antarctica 2019-01-19 to 2019-08-03, 11,879 nmi (22,000 km) in 196 days (saildrone.com, "Saildrone Completes First Autonomous Circumnavigation of Antarctica", read 2026-11-05; NOAA PMEL gives the same 22,000 km). `experiments/run_fielded_check.py` -> `results_fielded_check.txt` (instant).
- Prediction (informal): the fielded mission mean is below the model's buoy medians by a factor of 1.3 or more. Refuted otherwise.
- Result: held. Fielded mean 112 km/day (1.30 m/s). Model buoy medians were 237 to 272 km/day in January and 179 to 219 in July, so fielded/model is 0.41 to 0.63. At the 10 m/s typical of the Southern Ocean the model sits at its 303 km/day Froude cap, 2.7 times the fielded mean; the model reaches 112 km/day at a steady 2 m/s of wind.
- Reading: the model is an upper bound, about 2 to 3 times the one fielded figure I could source. The mission mean includes station keeping, surveying a route, 80 mph storms, drift and sail reefing, none of which the model has, and one drone is one data point. The ranking holds in direction: the fielded mean is still 1.3 times the 86 km/day solar ceiling, but that ceiling is itself an upper bound for the solar boat, so the gap between the two is not established to the factor 2 to 3 that 6h suggested.
- Limits: one vehicle, one mission, a mean over a mission rather than a daily distribution; a press-release figure, not a logged track; the Southern Ocean is not the buoy sites; the 7 m hull is not the 5 m model.
- Next: a daily-distance distribution from a public Saildrone mission track (NOAA PMEL ERDDAP data) in the buoy regions, then shrink the drone model by the measured factor and re-run 6h to 6j.

## 2026-11-05 · Milestone 6j: does 6i's wind-over-sun margin survive a tack-time penalty? (closes 6i's tack-penalty "Next"; prediction formed before the run, logged after)
- Why: 6i tacked instantly. `experiments/run_wind_vs_sun_tack.py` -> `results_wind_vs_sun_tack.txt` (47 s) multiplies the along-line speed of every tacking sample by L/(L+T), T = seconds lost per tack, L = seconds per leg (set by corridor width). Neither is sourced, so both are swept: (T, L) = (30,900), (60,900), (120,900), (60,300), (120,300), (120,120). Same buoy winds, four lines both ways, sail.py default drone, 86 km/day solar ceiling.
- Prediction (informal): with T = 60 s and L = 15 min the corridor/free ratio stays at 0.70 or more in both months and the January median stays above 2x solar. Refuted otherwise.
- Result: held. At (60, 900), January median 237 km/day (min 207), corridor/free 0.87; July 179 (min 114), 0.80. Harshest case (120 s lost every 2 min leg, 50% kept on tacking samples): January 220 (min 190), 0.80, all 9 buoy-months above 172 km/day; July 164 (min 104), 0.73, 3 of 10 above 172. Share of days at or above 86 km/day, July median 0.93 (min 0.57) there, against 0.97 (min 0.71) with free tacking.
- Reading: tack cost moves the corridor median by 1 to 8% in January and 1 to 9% in July across all cases (the largest in the harshest), because most hours at open-water buoys are sailed off the bow sector. The energy-source ranking from 6h/6i does not hinge on the tack model; the sun competes only in calm summer water, and a very tight corridor in a weak-wind month is where the margin thins (July minimum 104 km/day).
- Limits: tack loss modelled as a constant multiplier on tacking samples, not a manoeuvre simulation; T and L unsourced (swept); no leeway, heel, waves or gusts; two months; still no check against a fielded wind-driven vehicle's logged daily distance.
- Next: take a published daily distance of a fielded wind-driven vehicle (Saildrone, Wave Glider class) as the check; source a tack time.

## 2026-11-05 · Milestone 6i: does 6h's wind-over-sun margin survive a fixed survey line? (exploratory; the prediction below was formed before the run but not logged first)
- Why: 6h's free course ignores that a survey line fixes the heading, so wind straight on the bow costs the drone speed. `experiments/run_wind_vs_sun_corridor.py` -> `results_wind_vs_sun_corridor.txt` (about 1.5 minutes): same 10-minute NDBC buoy winds (speed and direction), lines at bearings 0, 45, 90, 135 deg each run both ways (lawnmower); direct sailing where the true wind angle is above the best-VMG angle, otherwise tacking between wind +-t_opt with along-line speed VMG/cos(th) (derived in the script, zero tack cost, so an upper bound). Same unsourced sail.py default drone.
- Prediction (informal): corridor km/day stays at 70% or more of the free course in every buoy-month, and the January median stays above 2x the 86 km/day solar ceiling. Refuted otherwise.
- Result: both held. Corridor over free course, median 0.88 (min 0.85) in January and 0.81 (min 0.77) in July. Median mean over the 4 lines: 240 km/day (min 209) January, 181 (min 115) July. Corridor mean exceeds 172 km/day (2x solar) in 15 of 19 buoy-months. Share of days at or above 86 km/day: median 1.00 (min 0.93) January, 0.97 (min 0.71) July.
- Reading: fixing the heading costs the drone 12 to 19% of its free-course distance, and the energy-source ranking from 6h stands; the sun competes only in calm summer water (the 0.71 and 115 km/day minima are the weak July buoys).
- Limits: zero tack cost and instant tacking (upper bound); buoy anemometer heights not corrected; one wind sample per 10 minutes taken as steady; two months; drone parameters unsourced; no check against a published daily distance of a fielded wind-driven vehicle yet.
- Next: take a published daily distance of a fielded wind-driven vehicle (Saildrone, Wave Glider class) to check the figure; add a tack-time penalty.

## 2026-11-05 · Milestone 6h: when does wind beat sun? The default wing-sail drone on real buoy winds against the solar boat's 86 km/day ceiling (exploratory, no prediction logged first)
- Why: 5n closed the archived-NWP branch; the notes said to return to the fleet comparison. `experiments/run_wind_vs_sun.py` -> `results_wind_vs_sun.txt` (under 1 minute): the default 5 m wing-sail drone (`sail.py`, every parameter swept elsewhere, none sourced) sails a free course (no corridor, no mark) on the 10-minute NDBC buoy winds already in `experiments/data` (9 buoys in January, 10 in July 2024). Speed at a beam reach (true wind angle 90 deg, my choice) and the best of 60 to 150 deg. The solar comparator is the 1.0 m/s best-range speed held for 24 h, 86 km/day, a ceiling because the real boat is also energy-limited (milestones 5j, 5m).
- Result: the drone reaches 1.0 m/s at 1.5 m/s of wind. Median km/day on a free course: 272 in January (245 to 282), 219 in July (149 to 275); best-angle choice adds under 3 km/day. The share of hours with the drone at or above 1 m/s is 0.93 to 1.00 in January and 0.75 to 0.99 in July (lowest: Gulf of Mexico buoy 42036 in July, mean wind 2.6 m/s). 100% of days clear 86 km/day at every buoy-month except 42036 July (0.83), 46013 July (0.94) and 46054 January (0.97).
- Reading: wherever there is open-ocean wind, the wind-driven drone's free-course distance is 2 to 3 times the solar boat's ceiling, so for a survey that does not need to hold a line the energy source, not the hull, decides the fleet ranking, and calm summer water (42036) is where the sun competes. The drone's number is a bound: the Froude cap (0.5, 3.5 m/s, 302 km/day) binds in windy buoys, buoy anemometers are at 4 to 5 m (reads low against sail height, which raises the drone), and heel, waves, leeway control, a course that must follow survey lines, and gusts are absent. It is not checked against a fielded drone's logged distance.
- Limits: unsourced drone parameters, beam-reach course, two months, 19 buoy-months, no sea state, no tack cost, no payload or hotel limit on the drone.
- Next: take a published daily distance of a fielded wind-driven vehicle to check the free-course figure; a survey-line (corridor) version using the 5c polar.

## 2026-11-05 · Milestone 5n: how much of HRRR's replanning gap is delivery lag? 1 h against 3 h latency on hourly cycles (closes 5k/5l's "Next"; pre-registration)
- Why: 5k to 5m left delivery latency untested. 5k used the 00/06/12/18z cycles with a 3 h latency, so the newest forecast at a replan was up to 9 h old. `experiments/fetch_hrrr_hourly.py` fetches every hourly cycle (`data/hrrr_buoyh_*.csv`; hourly cycles run to fhr 18, so forecast hours are 0,1,2,3,4,6,9,12,18 and the last value is held beyond 18 h) and `experiments/run_sail_hrrr_hourly.py` -> `results_sail_hrrr_hourly.txt` replans on the newest cycle issued at least L hours earlier, L = 1 and L = 3, on the same buoys, windows, polar, corridor and tau as 5k. Variants: raw and trailing-hour bias-corrected, at both L, with T0 and the header-follower as references.
- Predictions (written before the run; prior in brackets): P1 raw HRRR at L = 1 beats raw at L = 3 by at least 0.5 points in both months [45%]. P2 bias-corrected HRRR at L = 1 beats the header-follower by more than 0.5 points in at least one month [20%]. P3 raw at L = 3 on hourly cycles lands within 0.7 points of 5k's raw long-cycle rows (-0.8 Jan, -2.3 Jul) [65%, an implementation check]. Refuted if the outcome goes the other way.
- Limits (known before running): the 18 h forecast cap changes early-day replans (the tail of the window is held flat); two months, every 4th day; buoy anemometer heights not corrected (the bias-corrected rows absorb it); latency is the assumed delivery time, not a measured one.

### Outcome of 5n (`experiments/results_sail_hrrr_hourly.txt`, deterministic, 72 January and 80 July windows at 9 and 10 buoys, a few minutes; fetch of 3,900 hourly-cycle files took about 8 minutes, none missing)
- **P1 refuted:** raw HRRR at L = 1 minus L = 3 is +0.2 pts in January (boot 95% -0.1..+0.7) and +0.2 in July (-0.0..+0.4); neither reaches +0.5. Bias-corrected: -0.0 and +0.1.
- **P2 refuted:** bias-corrected L = 1 minus header-follower is +0.1 (Jan, -0.3..+0.4) and -0.6 (Jul, -1.1..-0.1). Raw L = 1: -0.7 and -1.7. Persistence T0: +0.0 and -0.2; the synthetic T = 120 min forecast stays +2.1 and +2.4.
- **P3 held:** raw L = 3 on hourly cycles is -1.1 (Jan) and -2.0 (Jul) against 5k's long-cycle -0.8 and -2.3, within 0.7 points.
- Reading: cutting delivery latency from 3 h to 1 h, and forecast age from up to 9 h to under 2 h, buys about 0.2 points. Delivery lag is not what separates archived HRRR from the 5d forecast; the lack of skill at 15-minute scale is. With 5h to 5m, archived 3 km HRRR at any correction, siting, height or latency tested does not beat following the headers. This closes the archived-NWP branch of the wing-sail sub-programme; the remaining forecast routes (radar or lidar nowcast, onboard sensing) cannot be tested with open data here.
- Limits: as pre-registered (18 h cap with a flat hold, two months, every 4th day, 1 h and 3 h are assumed latencies).
- Next: stop the wing-sail sub-programme on archived NWP and return to the milestone 6 fleet comparison.

## 2026-11-05 · Milestone 7r: 5l and 5m folded into the paper draft and README (no experiment run)
- Why: `paper/DRAFT.md` and the README stopped at 5k. Added a 5l/5m paragraph to each; every number is copied from the 5l and 5m entries and their result files.
- Limits: prose only; no new numbers.
- Next: hourly-cycle HRRR fetch (1 h latency); then stop the wing-sail sub-programme on archived NWP and return to the milestone 6 fleet comparison.
## 2026-11-05 · Milestone 5m: does correcting buoy winds to 10 m close 5k's raw-HRRR gap? (closes 5k's "Next"; exploratory, prediction informal)
- Why: 5k compared HRRR 10 m winds with buoy anemometers at 4 to 5 m, so part of HRRR's +0.6 to +0.9 m/s speed bias could be height. `run_sail_hrrr_buoy.py` now takes `BUOY_Z` (anemometer height, m) and scales observed speeds by the neutral log law to 10 m (z0 = 1.5e-4 m; factor 1.090 at 4 m, 1.067 at 5 m). Outputs: `experiments/results_sail_hrrr_buoy_z4.txt`, `_z5.txt` (about 3 minutes each). Heights are swept, not sourced per buoy (NDBC pages are not reachable from this session), so this is a bound on the height effect, not a station-exact correction.
- Expectation held before the run (informal): the speed bias shrinks to near zero and raw HRRR gains at least 0.5 points against the header-follower in July.
- **Result:** speed bias falls as expected (January +0.24 to +0.25, July +0.14 to +0.29 at 4 m; +0.39 to +0.41 and +0.26 to +0.40 at 5 m; 5k: +0.6 to +0.9). The ranking barely moves. Raw HRRR minus header-follower: -0.8 (Jan) and -1.9 (Jul) at 4 m, -1.0 and -1.7 at 5 m (5k uncorrected: -0.8 and -2.3). Bias-corrected HRRR: -0.1 and -1.0 at 4 m, -0.1 and -0.9 at 5 m (5k: +0.0 and -0.9). The synthetic 120 min forecast stays +2.0 to +2.5. The January gain was not met anywhere; the July gain was 0.4 to 0.6 (expectation half held at best, within bootstrap intervals).
- Reading: height explains part of the speed bias but almost none of the replanning gap. Direction error (24 to 30 degrees) and day-to-day shifts, which a height factor does not touch, carry it. 5k's conclusion stands: archived 3 km HRRR adds nothing over following the headers at these buoys.
- Limits: swept heights, one roughness length, neutral stability, same two months and windows as 5k.
- Next: shorter delivery latency (HRRR subhourly or 1 h) to see how much of the gap is lag; a nowcast built from persistence plus HRRR trend.
## 2026-11-05 · Milestone 5l: does HRRR's trend, added to the buoy's own level, beat persistence and the header-follower? (closes 5k's "nowcast" Next; pre-registration)
- Why: 5k found raw and bias-corrected HRRR no better than following the headers; the correction fails because the model's level and the day-to-day shift are both wrong. A nowcast keeps what the buoy knows (the level, from the trailing 30 min) and takes only the model's change since the replan, H(t) - H(k0), from archived HRRR (`experiments/run_sail_hrrr_trend.py` -> `results_sail_hrrr_trend.txt`; same buoys, windows, polar, corridor, tau, 3 h latency as 5k, no new fetch). Variants: trend1 (weight 1), trend05 (weight 0.5), trendfade (weight 1 decaying with lead, e-fold 120 min).
- Predictions (written before the run; prior in brackets): N1 trend1 beats persistence T0 by at least 0.5 points in both months [35%: a 3 km hourly model trend probably carries little of the 15-minute shifts]. N2 trendfade is at least as good as trend1 in both months [60%]. N3 no variant beats the header-follower by more than 1 point in either month [75%]. Refuted if the outcome goes the other way.
- Limits (known before running): same as 5k (two months, every 4th day, buoy heights not corrected to 10 m, but here only HRRR differences are used so the height offset cancels); weights and e-fold chosen by me, one setting each; 2-hourly forecast hours interpolated linearly.

### Outcome of 5l (`experiments/results_sail_hrrr_trend.txt`, deterministic, 72 January and 80 July windows at 9 and 10 buoys, a few minutes)
- **N1 refuted (no gain):** trend1 minus persistence T0 is +0.2 pts (Jan, boot 95% -0.1..+1.2) and -0.2 (Jul, -0.7..-0.0); neither reaches +0.5. trend05 is +0.3 (Jan, 0.0..1.2) and -0.0 (Jul).
- **N2 held:** trendfade is level with or above trend1 in both months (0.881 against 0.879 in January, 0.881 against 0.874 in July; tacks per day 118 against 146 in July).
- **N3 held:** no variant beats the header-follower by more than 0.1 pts (Jan +0.1, boot -0.2..+0.4); in July all are 0.3 to 0.6 below it.
- Reading: giving HRRR only the job of supplying the change, with the buoy supplying the level, removes the site mismatch and lifts HRRR from -0.8 / -2.3 (raw, 5k) to level with persistence, but not above it. A 3 km model's hourly-scale trend adds essentially nothing to what the last 30 minutes of observations already say. Together with 5h to 5k, archived HRRR at any of these correction levels does not carry the 15-minute skill that 5c/5d priced at about 3 points.
- Limits: as pre-registered; one weight and e-fold each; two months; open-water buoys only; HRRR cycles every 6 h with 3 h latency (a shorter latency still untested and needs a new fetch of hourly cycles).
- Next: hourly-cycle HRRR (latency 1 h) fetch; a wind-lidar or radar nowcast is untestable with open data here.

## 2026-11-05 · Milestone 5k: does archived HRRR lift replanning above the header-follower over open water (NDBC buoys)? (closes 5i/5j's "Next"; expectations written down after the run, not committed before it)
- Why: 5h to 5j found HRRR no better than following the headers at pier stations, where a 3 km cell and a pier anemometer mismatch. The NDBC host answers this session, so the same test runs at 11 open-water buoys (`experiments/fetch_ndbc_buoys.py` -> `experiments/data/ndbc_wind_*.csv`, `hrrr_buoy_*.csv`, `hrrr_buoy_cells.csv`; `experiments/run_sail_hrrr_buoy.py` -> `results_sail_hrrr_buoy.txt`, about 3 minutes). Same polar, corridor, tau, every 4th day, 3 h latency, hourly replan as 5h; 10-minute buoy series (window kept with 90% of 144 samples). Buoy-months used: 9 buoys in January, 10 in July (46042 has no 2024 file, 46026 has no July data, 41010 and 44025 none in January); 72 and 80 windows. Positions are from NDBC `station_table.txt`; buoy anemometers sit about 4 to 5 m up and are not corrected to 10 m.
- Expectations I held before the run (not in a committed file, so treat as informal): E1 raw HRRR within 2 points of the header-follower in both months; E2 raw HRRR above persistence; E3 the 1 h correction beats raw; E4 some HRRR variant beats the header-follower by 1 point; E5 HRRR mean absolute speed error under 1.5 m/s.
- **Result (negative again, gap smaller):** minus header-follower: raw HRRR -0.8 (Jan, boot 95% -3.8..-0.3) and -2.3 (Jul, -3.3..-1.0); bias-corrected HRRR +0.0 (-0.4..+0.4) and -0.9 (-1.6..-0.3); persistence T0 +0.0 and -0.2; the synthetic T = 120 min forecast of 5d +2.1 and +2.4. E1 held in January, failed in July. E2 failed (raw below persistence by 0.6 and 1.2). E3 held (+1.9, +0.7). E4 failed (best +0.0). E5 held in January (1.36 to 1.44 m/s), borderline-failed in July (1.42 to 1.52). HRRR direction error 24 to 30 degrees, speed bias +0.6 to +0.9 m/s, none growing with lead.
- Station spread: raw HRRR is far worse at three California buoys in January (46013 -12.5, 46054 -6.4, 46011 -4.6) and about level elsewhere; the correction removes most of that (-0.9, -0.3, -0.3).
- Reading: moving from piers to open water cuts the raw-HRRR penalty (-2.9 / -6.5 to -0.8 / -2.3) and the direction error (32-41 to 24-30 degrees), so part of 5h's loss was siting. The rest stays: with a trailing-hour correction HRRR only matches following the headers, so on these buoys it adds nothing a header-follower does not already have. The value found in 5c/5d needs forecast skill at 15-minute scale, which a 3 km hourly model plus a 3 h latency does not give. This does not test a boat in coastal or lee conditions, other models (a nowcast, radar, wind-lidar) or a shorter latency.
- Limits: two months, every 4th day, 72 and 80 windows from 9 to 10 buoys (stations differ between months, so the months are not strictly comparable); buoy heights not corrected to 10 m (raw rows only; the correction absorbs it); latency and 2-hourly forecast hours assumed as in 5h; buoys are 20 to 150 nm offshore, not coastal sailing.
- Next: a shorter latency (HRRR subhourly or 1 h) to see how much of the gap is delivery lag; correct buoy winds to 10 m; a nowcast-style forecast (persistence plus HRRR trend).

## 2026-11-05 · Milestone 5j: does a station bias learned from other days beat the trailing-hour correction on archived HRRR? (closes 5i's "Next"; exploratory, not pre-registered)
- Why: 5i left "a learned station-specific correction". `experiments/run_sail_hrrr_learned.py` -> `results_sail_hrrr_learned.txt` (deterministic, 96 windows per month at 12 stations, about 3 minutes, same archived files as 5h/5i, no new fetch). For each station-month, each window gets a speed ratio (mean observed / mean HRRR) and a circular-mean direction offset; the `learned` forecast applies the median ratio and circular-mean offset of the OTHER windows of that station-month (leave-one-window-out, held for the whole forecast). `both` averages learned and trailing-hour corrections. I wrote no predictions before running; my unrecorded expectation was that learned would beat bc1h, and it did not. The bc1h row reproduces 5i (-0.5 Jan, -3.0 Jul vs header).
- **Result (negative):** learned minus bc1h -0.6 pts (Jan, boot 95% -1.2..-0.3) and -1.6 (Jul, -2.8..-0.6); against the header-follower -1.0 (Jan) and -4.5 (Jul). Tacks per day rise to 264 / 452 (bc1h 172 / 291). `both` is -0.2 / -0.4 against bc1h. Median learned ratio 0.77 (Jan) and 0.76 (Jul) with a median offset magnitude of 16 deg: the stable part of the pier-versus-cell mismatch is a lower speed, but fixing it does not help; what hurts is the day-to-day wind shift, which a fixed correction cannot see.
- Reading: with 5h and 5i, no correction of 3 km HRRR (trailing hour, longer window, fade, or station-learned) lifts it above following the headers on these stations. Open-water winds (NDBC) remain the untested case; the host is refused by the proxy.
- Limits: leave-one-window-out uses windows from days after the test day, so it is an upper bound on what a deployed learner (past days only) could do; the result is still negative. Two months, piers and harbours, every 4th day, 8 windows per station-month to learn from; the speed ratio is clipped to 0.5 to 2.
- Next: open-water buoy winds if a host answers; spatial wind fields; stop correcting HRRR at the stations and test it where the boat sails.

## 2026-11-05 · Milestone 5i: does a longer or lead-fading bias correction lift real-HRRR replanning above the header-follower? (closes 5h's "Next"; pre-registration)
- Why: 5h's bias-corrected HRRR (speed ratio and direction offset from the trailing hour, held flat) recovered about 2 points but stayed 0.5 (Jan) and 3.0 (Jul) points below the header-follower. NDBC buoys (the open-water follow-up) are refused by the egress proxy (403 on www.ndbc.noaa.gov this session), so this step stays on the archived HRRR files of 5h (`experiments/data/hrrr_*.csv`, no new fetch). `experiments/run_sail_hrrr_bc.py` -> `results_sail_hrrr_bc.txt`: same windows (every 4th), polar, corridor, tau, 3 h latency as 5h; variants bc1h (5h's), bc3h (correction from the trailing 3 h) and fade (3 h correction plus the last 15 min correction decaying with lead, e-fold 120 min, so the near-term forecast is anchored on the newest observations), with persistence T0 and the header-follower as references.
- Predictions (written before the run; prior in brackets): Q1 bc1h reproduces 5h's hrrr_bc row to within 0.5 points [90%, implementation check]. Q2 bc3h beats bc1h by at least 0.5 points in both months [40%: a longer window averages out the 3 km cell mismatch but lags diurnal change]. Q3 fade beats bc3h by at least 0.5 points in both months [45%]. Q4 no variant beats the header-follower by more than 1 point in either month [75%]. Refuted if the outcome goes the other way.
- Limits (known before running): same 5h assumptions (station coordinates recalled, pier anemometers against 3 km cells, 3 h latency); the fade e-fold and window lengths are chosen by me, not tuned (one setting each, so no selection on the test windows); two months.

### Outcome of 5i (`experiments/results_sail_hrrr_bc.txt`, deterministic, 96 windows per month at 12 stations, about 4 minutes)
- **Q1 held:** bc1h reproduces 5h's bias-corrected row (-0.5 Jan, -3.0 Jul against the header-follower; 172 and 291 tacks a day).
- **Q2 refuted:** a 3 h trailing window is worse than 1 h, not better: bc3h minus bc1h -0.5 pts (Jan, boot 95% -0.7..-0.1) and -1.3 (Jul, -2.2..-0.9). The wind shifts faster than a 3 h average follows, so the longer window lags.
- **Q3 refuted, narrowly:** fade (3 h correction with the last 15 min correction decaying over 120 min) beats bc3h by +0.4 (Jan, 0.1..1.0, under the 0.5 line) and +2.0 (Jul); against bc1h it is +0.0 (Jan) and +0.6 (Jul, 0.1..1.2). So fade rescues the longer window but adds almost nothing over the plain 1 h correction.
- **Q4 held:** nothing beats the header-follower: best variant fade -0.7 (Jan) and -2.5 (Jul); persistence T0 is -0.2 and -2.6. Fade is level with persistence in July and 0.5 below in January.
- Reading: with a 3 km model cell compared against pier anemometers, bias correction brings archived HRRR to roughly the level of persistence and no higher; a fixed-length or fading local correction cannot lift it above following the headers. Together with 5h this closes off simple correction schemes on these stations. Whether HRRR carries skill over open water, where the boat would sail, is untested because NDBC is not reachable from this environment.
- Limits: as listed above; one setting per variant (no tuning); the stations are on piers and harbours.
- Next: open-water winds (NDBC buoys, if a host answers) against HRRR; a learned station-specific correction (regression on the previous days); spatial fields.

## 2026-11-05 · Milestone 5h: does 5d's value of forecast skill survive a real archived forecast (HRRR)? (pre-registration)
- Why: every "Next" since 5d. The HRRR archive (`noaa-hrrr-bdp-pds` on S3, GRIB2 byte ranges through the `.idx`) answered through the proxy this session, so the synthetic error model of 5d can be replaced by archived forecasts. `experiments/fetch_hrrr_wind.py` pulls the 10 m wind at the nearest 3 km cell to each of the 13 CO-OPS stations of 5a for every 4th day of Jan and Jul 2024 (the 5d windows). For a replan at UTC hour H the controller uses the newest long cycle (00, 06, 12, 18z) issued at least 3 h earlier (assumed latency), forecast hours every 2 h, linearly interpolated. `experiments/run_sail_hrrr.py` -> `results_sail_hrrr.txt` scores hourly replanning on the same windows, polar, corridor and tau as 5d with: raw HRRR; HRRR with a trailing-hour speed-ratio and direction-offset correction against the station (held for the whole forecast); and, on the same windows, 5d's persistence (T = 0) and T = 120 min rows plus the header-follower.
- Predictions (written before the run; prior in brackets): P1 raw HRRR beats persistence (T = 0) by at least 1 point in both months [60%]. P2 raw HRRR stays below 5d's T = 120 row (HRRR is hourly-smooth and cannot see the 15-minute shifts that carry 5c's gain) [70%]. P3 raw HRRR does not beat the header-follower by more than 1.5 points in either month [60%]. P4 the trailing-hour correction adds at least 0.5 points over raw HRRR [50%]. Refuted if the outcome goes the other way.
- Limits (known before running): station coordinates are the published CO-OPS values recalled to 0.01 deg, not looked up (the metadata API did not answer); the nearest HRRR cell at a coastal station can be land or water; CO-OPS anemometer heights differ from 10 m and are not corrected; the latency of 3 h and 2-hourly forecast hours are assumptions; only every 4th day is scored (8 days a month).

### Outcome of 5h (`experiments/results_sail_hrrr.txt`, deterministic, 96 windows per month at 12 stations, about 10 minutes; data `experiments/data/hrrr_*.csv`)
- **Raw HRRR loses to the header-follower, and to persistence:** minus header -2.9 pts (Jan, boot 95% -5.7..-1.6) and -6.5 (Jul, -8.0..-5.0); against 5d's persistence row it is -1.4 (Jan) and -2.4 (Jul) pts. Tacks per day 466 and 513 against 148 and 246 for persistence.
- P1 **refuted** (HRRR below, not above, persistence). P2 held (HRRR is 7.3 / 10.8 pts below T = 120, whose synthetic forecast is far better than a real one). P3 held, vacuously: HRRR does not beat the header-follower at all. P4 **held**: the trailing-hour speed-ratio and direction-offset correction adds +1.8 (Jan, 0.8..3.5) and +2.3 (Jul, 1.2..4.7) pts over raw, bringing it to -0.5 and -3.0 pts against the header-follower, about level with persistence in January and 0.4 below in July.
- HRRR against the stations: mean absolute speed error 1.7 to 2.0 m/s, mean absolute direction error 32 to 41 deg, speed bias +0.8 to +1.2 m/s, with no growth from the 0-3 h to the 6-12 h lead. The error is a site mismatch (3 km cell against a pier anemometer), not forecast decay; the raw rows are worst at San Francisco, Alameda and Crescent City (-10 to -21 pts in January).
- Reading: for a boat that replans from a gridded model forecast, the 5d result that value needs a forecast skilled at 15-minute scale holds with real data. An uncorrected 3 km model cell is worse than following headers; a local bias correction recovers about 2 points and no more. This does not say HRRR is a poor forecast over open water, where the boat would be; the stations are on piers and harbours.
- Limits: as listed above, plus the 3 h latency and 2-hourly interpolation assumptions; station coordinates not looked up; station-versus-cell mismatch confounds forecast skill with siting; one forecast model and two months.
- Next: bias correction that fades with lead or uses a longer window; open-water winds (NDBC buoys with HRRR) so the mismatch is smaller; spatial wind fields.

## 2026-11-05 · Milestone 7q: 5a to 5g folded into the paper draft and README (no experiment run)
- Why: the draft had no wing-sail section and the README stopped at 5d. Added section 2.5 to `paper/DRAFT.md` and a 5e to 5g paragraph to the README; every number is copied from the entries above and their `results_sail_*.txt` files.
- Limits: prose only; no new numbers. The synthetic forecast-error model of 5d is still the main gap.
- Next: archived HRRR or GFS winds if a host answers; spatial wind fields.

## 2026-11-04 · Milestone 5g: does a minimum-dwell rule rescue the low-skill rows of 5d? (closes 5f's "Next"; pre-registration)
- Why: 5e and 5f showed no proportional switch margin helps. Untested: forbid a voluntary tack within D minutes of the last tack (`tack.sim_replan(..., dwell=D)`; forced edge tacks still happen and reset the clock). Same windows (every 8th), polar, corridor, tau and forecasts as 5f; D = 0, 10, 20, 40, 80 min at T = 0, 30, 60, 120 min.
- Predictions (before running): D1 D = 0 reproduces 5f's margin-0 rows exactly (implementation check). D2 at T = 0 the best D cuts tacks per day by at least 40% against D = 0. D3 at T = 0 no D lifts progress to the header-follower's (5f found damping cannot fix persistence forecasts), so the T = 0 gap stays negative. D4 at T = 120 any D of 40 or more costs at least 0.5 points against D = 0.
- Limits: synthetic forecast error model of 5d; one dwell value for all conditions.

### Outcome of 5g (`experiments/results_sail_dwell.txt`, deterministic, 48 windows per month, about 20 minutes)
- **D1 held:** D = 0 rows equal 5f's margin-0 rows exactly in both months.
- **D2 half held:** at T = 0 the best D cuts tacks per day by 55% in January (166 to 74, D = 10) and 39% in July (248 to 152, D = 40), so July misses the 40% line narrowly.
- **D3 held:** at T = 0 every D loses to the header-follower, and more dwell is worse: January -1.8 to -2.8 points, July -2.6 to -3.9 (D = 0: -1.0, -2.2). Fewer tacks did not buy progress.
- **D4 held, more strongly:** at T = 120 D = 10 already costs 1.8 points (Jan) and 1.6 (Jul) against D = 0, D = 40 costs 4.3 and 5.5 points; D = 40 falls below the header-follower in both months.
- Reading: tack count is not the cost driver. Neither a proportional margin (5e, 5f) nor a minimum dwell (5g) rescues hourly replanning from a poor forecast; the better low-skill rule is the header-follower. Together these close off hysteresis-style damping of the replanner.
- Limits: as above; the tack penalty tau = 30 s is the only cost of a tack here.
- Next: archived HRRR or GFS winds if a host answers; spatial fields.

## 2026-11-04 · Milestone 5f: do finer switch margins (0.02 to 0.2) recover 5d's low-skill rows? (closes 5e's "Next") (pre-registration)
- Why: 5d's "Next". With forecast skill T of 120 min or less the hourly replanner tacks far more often than the header-follower (persistence: 148 to 246 tacks a day) and loses to it. Written in parallel with 5e (a sibling run landed it first, margins 0.5, 1, 2, same `margin` argument); this run was re-labelled 5f and keeps the finer grid 5e listed as its Next. Margin units as in 5e. Script `experiments/run_sail_margin_fine.py` -> `results_sail_margin_fine.txt`: T = 0, 30, 60, 120 min, margin 0, 0.02, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, every 8th window (half of 5d's, for run time), same data, polar, corridor, tau and error model as 5d. Margin is read off January and July is the holdout, as in 5b.
- Predictions (written before the run): M1 margin 0 reproduces 5d's rows on the shared windows to within 1 point (implementation check). M2 the best margin cuts tacks per day at T=0 by at least 50% against margin 0. M3 at T=0 the best margin lifts progress to at least the header-follower's in both months (prior 40%). M4 at T=120 the best margin adds under 1 point over margin 0 (the high-skill rows were not chattering). Refuted if the outcome goes the other way.
- Limits: same synthetic error model as 5d; margin is one scalar, not a hysteresis or minimum-dwell rule.

### Outcome of 5f (`experiments/results_sail_margin_fine.txt`, deterministic, 48 windows per month, about 9 minutes)
- **M1 held:** margin 0 matches 5d within 0.5 points on the halved window set (T=120: +3.4 Jan, +3.3 Jul against 5d's +3.0, +3.2; T=0: -1.0, -2.2 against -0.2, -2.6).
- **M2 refuted:** at T=0 no margin cuts tacks: 166 per day at margin 0 against 166 to 173 at 0.02 to 0.2 (January), 248 against 232 to 244 (July). The chatter is not near-ties that a margin would suppress; a persistence forecast produces edge-forced and large-gain switches.
- **M3 refuted:** at T=0 no margin reaches the header-follower: best -1.0 (Jan, margin 0) and -2.2 (Jul, margin 0); margins 0.02 to 0.2 are -1.1 to -1.5 and -2.5 to -2.6.
- **M4 held, more strongly:** at T=120 margin 0 is the best in July (+3.3) and within noise in January (+3.4); every margin of 0.1 or more costs 0.6 to 1.1 points. A margin never beat margin 0 by more than 0.2 points anywhere, and at T=30 and 60 it lost 0.4 to 2 points (T=30, margin 0.2: +0.2 Jan, -0.4 Jul).
- Reading: tack margin is not the missing piece for low-skill forecasts. Below about 30 minutes of skill the better rule is the header-follower itself, not a damped replanner. The 5d result stands as it was.
- Limits: as above; a minimum-dwell or hysteresis rule, not a proportional margin, is untested. Margins were not chosen on January and checked on July because none helped in either month.
- Next: archived HRRR or GFS winds if a host answers; a dwell-time rule; spatial fields.
